### Finding Verdicts

**heldRuns unguarded in tellDecks** — ADDRESSED. `hosted/infra/lib/handlers/ws.ts` (diff lines ~24-48 of the review diff): `tellDecks`'s entire body — the `decksOf` read, `const line = JSON.stringify({ t: "runs", runs: await heldRuns(sub) })`, and the post loop — is now inside one `try { … } catch (error) { console.error("live: could not say which runs are held", error); }`. No `await` in the function's happy path sits outside the guard, so a `manifest`/`watchers` rejection inside `heldRuns` can no longer escape `tellDecks`. Since `tellDecks` never rejects, `if (deck) await tellDecks(...)` in `$connect` no longer propagates a rejection, and `route()` still reaches `return { statusCode: 200 }`. `hosted/infra/test/ws.test.ts:227-251` adds `"still attaches a deck when its run list cannot be built"`, which overrides `store.manifest` to throw, drives a real `$connect` as a deck, and asserts `res.statusCode === 200` and `posted` is empty — proving both that the connect still succeeds and that no partial/garbage post went out. This matches the controller's ruling and the specific defect (manifest rejection escaping) is gone.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
None at Critical/Important severity. One behavioral note below (non-blocking, and it's the literal shape the controller's ruling mandated).

### Out-of-Scope Observations
- `hosted/infra/lib/handlers/ws.ts` (new `tellDecks` shape): wrapping the whole function body in one `try` instead of keeping the per-iteration `try/catch` around `poster.post`/`disconnect` inside the loop means that if posting to one deck's connection throws mid-loop, the remaining decks in that `decksOf(sub)` batch are skipped for this push (they'll only pick up the held-run list on the next change, not this one). This is a minor regression in fan-out coverage versus the pre-fix per-post isolation, but it is exactly the shape the controller's ruling specified ("the whole body of `tellDecks` goes inside a try/catch"), so it's not something the implementer introduced beyond the mandate — flagging only for awareness, not blocking.
- Test coverage under the fix targets `deps.store.manifest` rejecting; it does not add a second test for `deps.live.watchers` rejecting. The finding's "(or watchers)" phrasing reads as either being sufficient for one test, and `heldRuns`'s `await Promise.all(mine.map(... deps.live.watchers ...))` sits inside the same try as `manifest`, so the guard covers both paths identically — no functional gap, just noting the untested branch is structurally identical to the tested one.

### Verdict
**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage.</result>

This never forwards the press and never crashes the process, so it's fail-closed, but it's untested and diverges from the normal `refuse(...)` flow (deck gets a 500 instead of a `say`). This matches the existing convention in the same branch — `deps.store.getSession(run)` (`ws.ts:467`) is equally unguarded — so it's not a regression introduced by this task, just an untested edge case worth a follow-up test if store failures matter here.
- `ws.test.ts:425-439` ("lets a deck watch without Plus") can't regress-detect gating on `$connect` specifically, since `entitled` is never referenced on that path — it would pass identically whether or not the fix existed. This is copied verbatim from the brief, so not an implementer defect, just a weak assertion worth flagging.

### Strengths
- The press mapping was genuinely read off the page rather than guessed, and four of six arms match exactly. Checked one by one: `roll` (`RunView.tsx:385-391`) reproduces `StepPanel`'s button at `:1436-1441` including the `keyPrefix` — `key` is `${phase.id}#${index}` (`:1412`) so `u${state.unit}:${key}` and `u${unit}:${phase.id}#${index}` are the same string; `carry-on` → `run.completeStep(phase, index)` matches `:1509` for the non-closing manual case; `close` → `run.closeAndEnter(phase, index)` matches `ClosingStep`'s primary at `:1664`; `enter` → `run.enterUnit()` matches both `:784` and `:1729`; `undo` → `run.undo()` matches `:1318`; `move` → `run.takeMove(m.id, m.label)` matches `:2242`.
- The report's correction of the brief's one-argument `declareSubject` to the real `(phase, index, subjectType)` (`useRun.ts:502`) is right, and it sources `phase`/`index` from `run.activeStep` as the page's own Declare button does (`:1482`).
- `close`'s page-side gates really are pre-guaranteed: `ClosingStep`'s `blocked` is `run.blockingObligations` (`:1604`) which `offerOf` refuses via `input.owed &gt; 0`, and its `points` are `step.confirm` which `offerOf` refuses when non-empty — so `allTicked` is trivially true when the offer hands out `close`.
- Risk 2 (the `drove` route) clears: `sync.drove(news.from, news.ref, ...)` (`RunView.tsx:409`) sits *inside* the `news.run !== runId` guard at `:371`, so a reply is never sent for a run this page is not holding; `to`/`ref` are the incoming drive's own; `SyncProvider.tsx:241` forwards to the same `socketRef.current` that `openLive` created and that delivered the drive. The socket test asserts the exact `{"t":"drove","to","ref","ok","say"}` frames.
- Risk 3 (seq staleness) clears: `useRun` returns a fresh object literal (`useRun.ts:1108`), so the effect re-runs every render and the closure always holds the current `run`. `currentOffer`'s deps cover every field `offerOf` reads — `events.length`, `started`, `readOnly`, `state`, `pending`, `activeStep`, `moves`, `canUndo`, `blockingObligations.length`, `receipts.length`, `pack` — and I checked the identity-stability trap the new publish-effect dep could have opened: `state` (`useRun.ts:213`), `activeStep` (`:422`), `moves` (`:605`) and `blockingObligations` (`:1103`) are all `useMemo`'d, so `currentOffer` does not churn per render and the 800 ms debounce at `RunView.tsx:265` is not perpetually restarted.
- Risk 4 (`seen`) clears: the clearing effect is declared before the subscribing one, so on a run-id change React runs the clear first; and the `news.run !== runId` filter means a run-A ref cannot even reach `takePress` while run B is held.
- Tests assert both halves — every case checks the `Verdict` and whether the `vi.fn()` act was or was not called (`takePress.test.ts:17-18`, `:24-25`, `:32-33`, `:42-43`).
- The two out-of-list edits are genuinely minimal and behavior-preserving. `LibraryView.tsx:97` narrows `news.kind === "run"` to the two variants that carry a `Kind`; I checked every emitted gesture kind (`gestures.ts:84,99,121,150,162,171,175` plus `"rolled"`/`"setup"` at `RunView.tsx:468,492,862`) and none is `"run"`, so nothing that used to reload stops reloading. `Members.test.tsx:58` adds one mock field.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **`carry-on` drives the wrong function on a unit-closing manual step.** `offer.ts:81-85` hands out `primary: { id: "carry-on" }` for *any* `manual` step with an empty checklist, including one with `closesUnit: true`. But the page routes that exact step to `ClosingStep` (`RunView.tsx:1490-1492`, via `closesUnit` at `packages/engine/src/flow.ts:123-125`), whose button calls `run.closeAndEnter` (`:1664`) — not `completeStep`. The deck's `carry-on` arm calls `run.completeStep(active.phase, active.index)` (`RunView.tsx:393`). The two are not equivalent: `closeAndEnter` writes `stopClocksEvents` + `UnitFinalized` + step completion + `UnitEntered` + the next unit's clock (`useRun.ts:522-536`), while `completeStep` writes only the step-completion events. A deck press would leave the run with a closed step and no `UnitFinalized`/`UnitEntered` — a state the page itself can never produce. Compounding it, that manual branch returns before `offer.ts:92`'s `input.owed &gt; 0` check, so the press also bypasses `ClosingStep`'s "Settle what is owed first" gate (`RunView.tsx:1661-1662`). The offer's shape predates this task, but this task is what makes it press a button; and `offer.ts` is no longer off-limits, since the fix round already amended it. Fix at the source (`offer.ts` should emit `close` for any `closesUnit(step)`, and apply the `owed` check before the manual branch) or route `carry-on` through `closesUnit` in the act.
2. **An `answer` press is taken against an offer that is not asking for one** (plan-mandated, labeled). `takePress.ts:60-64` accepts `press: "answer"` whenever `press.answer` is a non-empty object; it never consults `at.offer.presets` or `at.offer.primary`. The act (`RunView.tsx:403-406`) only guards `if (!active) return`. So with a `rollTable` step active at seq N, a deck sending `{press:"answer", answer:{x:1}}` gets `{ok:true}` and `run.declareSubject(phase, index, "")` runs — which is `completeStep(phase, index, [{t:"SubjectDeclared", subjectType:""}])` (`useRun.ts:502-505`), completing the roll step without ever rolling and writing an empty subject the page's own Declare button refuses (`disabled={!declared}`, `RunView.tsx:1482`). The brief's Step 3 omitted the check, so this is the plan's defect as much as the implementer's, but the shipped code is what a deck can reach. Gate the `answer` arm on `at.offer.presets` carrying a `declareSubject` preset, and treat a blank `subject` as `{ ok:false, say:"That answer was empty." }`.
3. **A deck can take a finalizing move the page disables.** `currentOffer.moves` is `run.moves.map(m =&gt; ({ id, label }))` unfiltered (`RunView.tsx:207`), and `takePress` allows anything in `offer.moves` (`takePress.ts:50-54`). The page's own move button is `disabled={held}` where `held = Boolean(move.finalizes) &amp;&amp; owed &gt; 0` (`RunView.tsx:2236,2242`). So with an obligation outstanding, a deck can press a unit-closing move the page refuses. Same class as #1: the offer's mapping predates the task, the pressability is new. Filter `held` moves out of the offer's `moves`.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- **A throwing act eats the verdict and re-arms the press.** `takePress.ts:41,52,58,62` call `act.*()` *before* `settle(...)`, so an exception escapes with the `seen` entry unwritten — `sync.drove` (`RunView.tsx:409`) is never reached (the deck waits on silence) and a retry with the same `ref` re-attempts the act, which is a double-press path if the act was partly applied. It also starves the rest of the bus: `bus.ts:30-32` is a bare `for (const l of listeners) l(news)` with no try/catch, so later subscribers miss that news. Settling before acting, or wrapping the act, would close both.
- **`between`'s `pending` gate is stricter than the page's.** `RunView.tsx:229` uses `run.pending === null`; the page's ternary tests `run.pending?.request` (`:777`). A run with a non-null `pending` that carries no `request` still shows its Enter button but offers the deck nothing. Conservative, not a false-allow, and consistent with `offerOf`'s `settled` input — worth a line of comment rather than a change.
- **`seen` is keyed on `ref` alone.** `takePress.ts:27` ignores `press.from`, so two decks using colliding ref strings (a per-deck counter, say) would have the second deck's first press answered with the first deck's verdict and nothing pressed. The brief specified ref-only keying; a `${from}:${ref}` key costs nothing.
- **`driveSeen` grows without bound** for the life of a run id (`RunView.tsx:356-359`). Harmless at deck volumes, but nothing ever evicts.
- **The drive effect re-subscribes on every render** (`RunView.tsx:412` deps include the whole `run`, a fresh literal per `useRun.ts:1108`). Not a leak and not a double-press — React runs the cleanup before the new subscribe, and only one listener is ever registered — merely a Set delete/add per render. The report's concern 4 is accurate and the honest call was to follow the brief.
- **Concern 2 of the report is confirmed and unfixed:** a per-contestant move (`RunView.tsx:2262`, four arguments including `c.id`) cannot be driven, because `Offer.moves` carries no contestant. Correctly scoped to the offer's shape, not this task.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** The wiring, routing, `seq` discipline and `seen` handling are all correct and carefully sourced from the page's own buttons, but three press paths let a deck do something the page's own button refuses or does differently — a unit-closing manual step driven by `completeStep` instead of `closeAndEnter`, an `answer` press accepted against a roll step, and a held finalizing move — and the first two can leave the run in a state the page cannot produce.</result>

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **Plan-mandated defect, labeled:** `useToast`'s effect keys off `text` (`Toast.tsx:14-19`), so calling `show()` twice with the *same* string is a no-op re-render in React (`Object.is` bail-out) — the timeout is not reset, violating the stated primitive contract "a second `show` restarts it." This is inherited verbatim from the brief's own Step-3 reference code (report confirms it's an exact copy), not an implementer deviation, but it's still a defect in the shipped primitive and untested (`Toast.test.tsx` only exercises a single `show`). It happens not to manifest at the one call site in `RunView.tsx` (each `toast.show` call there carries a distinct count string, and `hadDecks` prevents repeat calls at the same count), so it is latent rather than currently reachable — but `useToast` is an exported, reusable primitive and the bug will resurface the moment a second caller repeats identical text.
- **No integration test for the toast's actual trigger inside `RunView`:** the task's headline behavior — the table sees a note pop up when `decks` rises — is proven only in pieces (`Toast.test.tsx` tests the primitive with a manually-clicked button; `RunView.offer.test.tsx`'s new tests assert the Attached-panel row, not the toast). No test emits a `tools` gesture that raises `decks` while `RunView` is mounted and checks for `screen.getByRole("status")` or the exact copy `"A Stream Deck is on this run."` This leaves the wiring at `RunView.tsx:187-197` — the actual feature commit is named for — unverified end-to-end.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `Attached` (`RunView.tsx:2111`) has no internal null-guard for `tools.length === 0 &amp;&amp; decks === 0`; it relies entirely on the caller's gate at `RunView.tsx:849`. Harmless today (single call site, confirmed via `grep -n "&lt;Attached" RunView.tsx`), but the component's own contract ("returns null with no tools and no decks") is implicit rather than enforced, so a future second call site could silently render an empty panel (just the trailing `&lt;p className="muted small"&gt;` line) with no heading.
- The rise-only toast logic interacts with `useAttachedDecks`'s existing reset-to-0-on-`runId`-change behavior (`useAttachedTools.ts:59`, unchanged by this diff): since `RunView` is keyed by `pack.id` not `runId` (`App.tsx:1345`), `hadDecks` (`RunView.tsx:194`) is not itself run-scoped — it is only incidentally zeroed via the transient `decks→0` render that `useAttachedDecks` produces on every run switch. This does make "switch to a 2-deck run, then a 1-deck run" stay silent through the transient zero and then correctly re-fire on subsequent rises (including back up to 2), so the mechanism holds together, but it also means switching into any run that already has a deck attached will announce "A Stream Deck is on this run." even though nothing just arrived — a quirk worth a code comment, not a fix, since it flows directly from `useAttachedDecks`'s own no-resync design (pre-existing, Task 6) rather than something Task 10 introduced.
- Copy audit clean: no British spellings found in the added copy/comments; "A Stream Deck is on this run." reads as plain status, consistent with `status-copy-is-plain` and `no-preaching-in-ui` house rules (memory-file context, not this diff's own constraint list, but worth noting it doesn't conflict).

### Strengths
- **Risk 1, key parity is right for the ordinary case.** `Checklist.tsx:167-168` reproduces the page's box exactly — `shown.length &gt; 0 ? children : [\`${i}\`]` mirrors `Checklist.tsx:87` (`toggle(shown.length &gt; 0 ? childKeys : [\`${i}\`], …)`), the `!ticked.has(k)` filter at `:169` mirrors `toggle`'s own filter at `:76`, and the skip at `:166` calls `pointMade` rather than restating "a point with evidence is made when every row is". A point that shows a table with nothing on it is skipped because `pointMade` returns `true` for it (`evidence.ts:107`), which is the same reason the card draws no row.
- **Tally arithmetic matches a click.** `check` moves the counter by `items.length` (`useRun.ts:738`), so one call per tally group equals N single-box clicks. Confirmed at the source, not taken from the report.
- **Deviation (a) is a correctness improvement the ruling should have included.** Without `settling`, `ticksToFinish` on a closing card would emit keys for rows where `rowMade` is already `true` via `settled` (`evidence.ts:112`) and for rows the card disables as `owing` — extra `Checked` events, and a counter moved past what any click could move it. Keeping the fourth-argument calls working means the brief's own tests still read as written. Accept it.
- **Deviation (b) is honest enough for a key face.** `items` is a hint beside a label that already says what the press does; the press itself ticks only what `ticksToFinish` returns. It can only over-count (a point showing an empty table, or a hidden constraint row), never under-count, and the trade is disclosed in the comment above `offer.ts:114`. Widening `OfferInput` to carry pack and log for a count would have cost `offerOf`'s purity. Accept it.
- **Tests assert keys and groups, not counts.** `Checklist.test.tsx:64`, `:68`, `:73`, `:80`, `:84`, `:88` are whole-array `toEqual`s including the tally on its own group, and `:91-105` clicks the real checkbox and compares the helper against what `onToggle` received — the one test that can catch drift.
- **The `RunView.offer.test.tsx` addition is minimal and real**: an optional `rest(at)` on the existing fixture (`:214`, `:220`), one assertion that `needsPage` survives beside the preset (`:262-267`). Nothing else in the file moved.
- Checked that no schema strips the new field in transit: `presets` appears nowhere outside `offer.ts`, `takePress.ts` and these tests, so `items` is not validated away between the page and a deck.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
- None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **A hidden constraint row is ticked under the point's tally, where the page's own control ticks it without one.** `Checklist.tsx:168` filters `shown` by `owing`/`settled` only, while the card filters `listed` first (`Checklist.tsx:69-72`), and `listed` drops rows where `hidden(s) &amp;&amp; answerable(s)` (`Checklist.tsx:68-69`). For a closing **manual** step with `constrainedBy`, `settlingFor` marks every row whose result is a constraint line `hidden` and every row a point shows `answered` (`owed.ts:115-118`), so such a row is not in `childKeys`: on the page it is ticked by the Constraints panel's "Honored" box, which calls `tick(boxes, !on)` with **no tally** (`RunView.tsx:1720`). The helper puts the same key into the point's tally group instead. Same keys, different counter: where such a point carries `tally`, one deck press moves the counter further than any sequence of clicks can. Including the row at all is correct and necessary — `pointMade` still waits on it (`evidence.ts:107`), so filtering it out would make the press throw on every such step — so the fix is the tally attribution, not the key set. Narrow (closing manual step + `constrainedBy` + a tallied point showing that same table), and no test covers it: the click-comparison test at `Checklist.test.tsx:91` passes no `settling` and its fixture has no tally.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `RunView.tsx:129` returns silently when `run.state` is null, so `takePress` settles `{ ok: true }` for a press that did nothing. `active` being non-null makes it unreachable in practice; a throw would fail closed the way the rest of the function does.
- The catch's passthrough (`takePress.ts:63`) cannot carry a store error, but it will carry a `TypeError` from a future act — a deck would see "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'phase')" on a key face. Narrowing to a tagged error, or a `say` only for errors the act means to show, would keep internals off the face. Worth the line in Task 11's docs pass the report proposes either way.
- One press writes one `check` move per tally group plus the finish move, so undoing it takes several presses of Undo. That matches clicking the boxes one at a time, so it is consistent rather than wrong — just worth knowing.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** Every ruling is implemented as written, the done-check provably precedes any commit, and `ticksToFinish` calls the evidence machinery rather than restating it; both reported deviations hold up on the merits. The one real divergence from a click — a hidden constraint row carried into a tally group — is narrow enough to fix as a follow-up predicate rather than a blocker.</result>

### Strengths
- Every wire field, refusal sentence, and offer field named in the docs was traced to an exact source location and matches verbatim — no fabricated fields or strings found anywhere in the new section.

- Script never logs the token or URL, and only ever writes via `drive`, matching the proof's safety constraints.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- **`RUNLOG_WS`'s dev address is not documented anywhere in the shipped deliverable**, contradicting controller ruling 4 ("the dev address is documented"). Neither `docs/stream-api.md` nor `scripts/drive.ts` mentions `RUNLOG_WS` or `wss://runlog.dev.scrthq.com` (confirmed via grep, zero matches in both files). It exists only in the task report, which is excluded from documentation by the very markdownlint-ignore this PR adds. Fix: add a one-line note, either in the new doc section or as a comment beside `drive.ts:13`.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `scripts/drive.ts` has no `close`/`error` listener on the socket (`drive.ts:28-42`). If the connection drops, a subsequent Enter press calls `ws.send` on a closed socket, which is silently ignored per the WebSocket spec — the user gets no feedback that the press went nowhere. The brief's own Step 1 sample has the same gap, so this isn't a new regression, but it's worth a one-line `ws.addEventListener("close", ...)` given the proof is meant to model what the real plugin will do.
- The deck's tracked `seq` starts at `0` and is only corrected by a `changed` message; for a run already in progress, the very first `changed` arrives via the page's debounced snapshot republish (triggered by the deck-count change, `RunView.tsx:356`'s `decks` dependency → `notify` at `hosted/infra/lib/handlers/api.ts:2690`), which converges within about a second — consistent with the brief's own "within a second" framing. Traced through and found not to be a defect, but the mechanism is indirect enough to be worth a short comment in the script explaining why `seq` starts at `0` rather than looking uninitialized.

**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** The implementation is unusually faithful to the wire protocol — every documented field, refusal string, and message shape checks out exactly against `ws.ts`, `takePress.ts`, `offer.ts`, and `socket.ts` — but it misses one explicit controller ruling (the dev `RUNLOG_WS` address must be documented, and it currently isn't anywhere in the shipped files), which is a small, concrete gap to close before approval.</result>

<result>### Finding Verdicts

- **Important — dev `RUNLOG_WS` address documented nowhere shipped.** — ADDRESSED. `docs/stream-api.md:27` (diff) now reads "...never a deck. The same address answers on the dev copy, at `wss://runlog.dev.scrthq.com`." — exact address, no invention. `scripts/drive.ts:13` adds `// RUNLOG_WS=wss://runlog.dev.scrthq.com reaches the dev copy instead.` directly above the `const base = ...` default (`scripts/drive.ts:14`). Implementer's report shows `lint:docs` clean (0 issues / 20 files).

- **Minor — a press on a dropped socket went nowhere silently.** — ADDRESSED. `scripts/drive.ts:34-37` adds a `close` listener that sets `connected = false` and prints `"socket closed"`; `scripts/drive.ts:38` adds an `error` listener printing one line. `scripts/drive.ts:55-56` guards the stdin handler: `if (!connected) return console.log("not connected");` before any `ws.send`, so a press on a closed socket no longer calls `send`.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff

- Minor — `connected` (scripts/drive.ts:32) is initialized `true` at declaration and only ever cleared by the `close` listener; it is never set by an `open` event and the guard never consults `ws.readyState`. A press struck before the socket actually reaches `OPEN` (i.e., during the `CONNECTING` window right after the script starts) will pass the `connected` check and call `ws.send()` on a not-yet-open socket, which the WebSocket spec (and undici's implementation, per `node_modules/undici-types/websocket.d.ts`) makes throw `InvalidStateError`. This is the same gap the review's Scope note called out as the alternative-acceptable pattern ("an open flag set on open/cleared on close") — the fix implements only the "cleared on close" half. Note this specific race pre-dates this diff (the base script called `ws.send()` unconditionally too), so it isn't a regression the diff introduced, but the diff had the opportunity to close it fully and didn't; flagging since Scope explicitly asked this be checked.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

- `scripts/drive.ts` is not included in any of the six `tsc --noEmit -p ...` projects run by `npm run typecheck` (checked `package.json`'s `typecheck` script and the referenced tsconfigs) — the file is only syntax-checked (`node --experimental-strip-types --check`), not type-checked, by any of the four commands the implementer ran. Pre-existing, unrelated to this fix diff (the typecheck script itself is unchanged).
- The script's header comment says "about forty lines"; the file is now 68 lines total (59 before this fix round, +9 from the diff). Growth from this diff is modest (close/error handling + one comment) and directly serves the reviewed finding; the larger drift from "~forty" predates this round.
- `(event as ErrorEvent).message ?? String(event)` (scripts/drive.ts:38): per `undici-types/websocket.d.ts`, the WebSocket `error` event is typed as `ErrorEvent` with a non-optional `message: string`, so the cast is accurate and the `?? String(event)` fallback is effectively dead code — harmless, not a bug.

### Verdict

**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage. One Minor gap noted above (send during the `CONNECTING` window isn't guarded), pre-existing and not introduced by this diff.</result>

<summary>Agent "Final whole-branch review: the deck drives" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Read the plan (both halves), the ledger in full, and the 3,882-line diff in eight passes, then verified the load-bearing seams against the working tree (`useRun.ts`, `RunView.tsx` button handlers, `store.ts` seq, `api-stack.ts` grants and env).

### Strengths

- **The seq contract actually holds.** `store.ts:798,815` hands out one seq per event via an atomic `ADD`, the log is append-only (undo appends `Undone`, `useRun.ts:845`, rather than dropping events), and `SEEN#` dedupes replays — so the server's counter and the page's `run.events.length` are the same number, including events appended by another member's device. The stale-press refusal is sound, and it fails in the safe direction in every lag case I could construct.
- **The act map was verified against the page, key by key.** `RunView.tsx:456-480` builds the roll block with `keyPrefix: u${unit}:${phase.id}#${index}` — byte-identical to the page's own button (`RunView.tsx:1521`) and to the hands-free path (`:617`). `between`'s words match `StartFirstUnit` (`:1459`) and `BetweenUnits` (`:1814`) exactly; `finishWords` is now the one spelling behind both the closing card's button and the offer. `run.check(key, items, on, tally)` matches the page's `tick` argument for argument, and `commit` uses a functional `setEvents` (`useRun.ts:286`), so the several-groups-then-finish sequence in `tickEverythingAndFinish` cannot lose a tick.
- **The Task 9 fix round was the right call and landed correctly.** Routing `close` off `closesUnit(step)` rather than `step.kind`, hoisting the owed check above the manual branch, gating `answer` on a preset, and keying `seen` on `${from}:${ref}` all narrow a deck to what the page allows. `takePress` is genuinely pure and the tests are behavioral.
- **`offTheList` as one shared predicate** (`Checklist.tsx:20`) is the right shape for the Task 12 finding, and the two "commits what a click commits" tests (`Checklist.test.tsx`) compare `ticksToFinish` against a real `fireEvent.click` on the same box — that is the strongest test in the branch.
- **One entitlement check, one feature parser.** `grantsOf`/`featuresFromEnv` in `billing.ts`, imported by both handlers, with `api.ts`'s inline copy deleted. The live Lambda does get `RUNLOG_GATES`/`STRIPE_FEATURES` (`api-stack.ts:461-462`) and already had `grantReadWriteData` (`:479`), so the deploy works.
- The `memoryLive` two-row rewrite (`ws.test.ts`) is a real fidelity improvement; the Task 6 concern that produced it was caught the right way.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)

**1. A press is applied once per device holding the run — duplicate moves, and a write from a device that is not the owner's.**
`hosted/infra/lib/handlers/ws.ts:477,491` — `writers` is *every* watcher passing `writes()`, and the press is posted to all of them. `writes()` excludes links, scenes, tools and decks, but not the owner's second device and not another member's browser. On the page side nothing narrows it either: `RunView.tsx:447` gates only on `publishing`, and `decks` reaches every watcher via the `tools` gesture, so every member's page subscribes and acts.

Consequences, in order of how likely they are:
- Streamer with the run open on a laptop and a phone: one deck press → two `run.begin(...)` → two rolls in the log, both syncing. Data corruption.
- A co-player with the run open: their device applies the owner's deck press and authors the event, which directly breaks the binding constraint "a deck press lands in the log as an ordinary move by the owner's device". Their `drove` reply is then silently dropped at `ws.ts:522` (`target.sub !== conn.sub`), so the owner's deck may also get the wrong verdict or none.

Fix: choose one target server-side. `const writers = (await deps.live.watchers(run)).filter(writes).filter((w) =&gt; w.sub === conn.sub)` restores the ownership constraint; then take exactly one — sort by connection id (or add `connectedAt` to the watch row and take the newest) and `slice(0, 1)`. Add a ws test with two pages asserting one `drive` is posted, and one with a second member's connection asserting it is not posted at all. This is a plan defect, not an implementation slip: Task 7 specified the fan-out and the pre-flight scan marked T3↔T7 "agree".

#### Important (Should Fix)

**2. `moves` and `undo` escape the `live` gate.**
`apps/web/src/run/offer.ts:69-72` — `bare` is built before the liveness check and carries `moves` and `undo` into the `!live` return. `takePress`'s `move`/`undo` arms consult only `offer.moves`/`offer.undo`, never `needsPage`. So on a device where `live` is false — a viewer's page (`readOnly`), which `canUndo` (`useRun.ts:857`) and `moves` (`:605`) do not consider — a deck press of `move` or `undo` is accepted and written (`commit`/`begin`/`undo` have no `readOnly` guard). Combined with #1, this is the mechanism by which a viewer's browser writes to someone else's run. Fix: return `{ ...bare, moves: [], undo: null, primary: null, needsPage: "Open the run on the page" }` on the `!live` branch, with a test.

**3. Per-contestant moves are offered, and pressing one does something the page cannot do.**
`RunView.tsx:283` maps every `run.moves` entry; nothing filters `move.per === "contestant"`. The page splits exactly there (`Moves`, `RunView.tsx:2325`): with a roster it renders one button per contestant and calls `run.takeMove(id, label, undefined, contestant)`. The deck's act calls `run.takeMove(m.id, m.label)` with no contestant, recording a per-racer move against the table — "one death counter between four people", in `useRun.ts`'s own words. The ledger records this as "per-contestant moves cannot be driven"; they can, wrongly. Fix: `.filter((m) =&gt; m.move.per !== "contestant" || !run.moderated || run.state!.contestants.length === 0)` beside the `heldMove` filter.

**4. A page that opens or reloads a run after the deck attached never learns the deck is there — and then silently ignores every press.**
`tellWhoIsAttached` fires only on a tool's hello (`ws.ts:296`), a control/deck disconnect (`:244,247`) and a deck's watch (`:656`). It never fires when a *page* watches (`:651`). So: deck watches s1 → page reloads (F5 mid-stream) → page comes back with `decks = 0` → on an unshared run `publishing` is false → `RunView.tsx:447` returns early → presses are forwarded by the server, nobody subscribes, no `drove` is ever sent, and the deck's key hangs with no error anywhere. Same for opening the run after the deck picked it. Fix (one line): call `await tellWhoIsAttached(m["id"])` for every watcher joining, not just a deck — it also fixes the Attached panel missing a tool after a reload. And belt: drop `publishing` from the drive-effect gate at `:447`; `currentOffer` is computed unconditionally and already answers `needsPage`, so the page holding the run should always answer a press.

**5. A press can go unanswered three ways, and nothing in the wire times out.**
Besides #4: (a) a page that leaves the run for the library keeps its `WATCH#` row — `socket.ts:169` sends nothing for `watch(null)` and there is no unwatch message — so `heldRuns` still reports the run held, the press is forwarded to a page with no `RunView` mounted, and nothing replies; (b) `deps.store.getSession` or `deps.entitled` rejecting throws past the `drive` branch to the handler's catch (`ws.ts:~690`), returning 500 with no `drove`. `docs/stream-api.md` promises a verdict for every press. Fix: wrap the drive branch's store/entitlement reads so a failure becomes `refuse("Could not check that right now.")`, and document a client-side timeout (the plugin will need it regardless).

**6. The deck's only source of `seq` lags the page by the sync settle, so the second press in ~2s is refused.**
The `changed` doorbell carries `meta.seq`, the server's event count, which only advances after the sync push (`SyncProvider` settles 2000ms). A press lands, the page moves to N+1, the deck still holds N until the push — every press inside that window answers "That moved on.". The `drove` verdict is the obvious carrier for the new seq and currently carries none (`socket.ts` `drove(to, ref, ok, say)`). Decide this before Plan B builds a follow key on top of it; a human pressing Enter in `scripts/drive.ts` and reading the output takes longer than 2s, which is why Task 11's live check would not have surfaced it.

**7. The test that pins the live Lambda's gate env passes for the wrong reason.**
`hosted/infra/test/api.test.ts:112-124` matches any function with `WS_ENDPOINT` + `RUNLOG_GATES` + `STRIPE_FEATURES`. `WS_ENDPOINT` is set on the API handler (`api-stack.ts:566`) and the Discord job (`:568`) as well as the live handler (`:572`), and the API handler already had the other two (`:222,224`). Delete `api-stack.ts:461-462` and this test still passes — the one thing that would make the gate inert on deploy is unguarded. Fix: pull `template.findResources("AWS::Lambda::Function")`, find the entry whose logical id starts with `LiveHandler`, and assert on that resource's env.

**8. A deck that switches runs leaves the old page publishing forever.**
`ws.ts:651-657` tells the new run's table, and `$disconnect` (`:247`) tells only `leaving.run`, the last one. The stale `WATCH#` row for run A is never recomputed, so A's page stays at `decks = 1`, keeps `publishing` on, and keeps PUTting snapshots of an unshared run for nobody until it is remounted. The ledger accepts "keep publishing after the deck leaves" for the toast; this is the broader version. Fix: capture `conn.run` before the re-connect and `tellWhoIsAttached(previous)` when it differs.

**9. The act map has no automated coverage at all.**
`RunView.tsx:447-494` is where three of the branch's Important findings lived, and `RunView.offer.test.tsx` never emits a `{ t: "drive" }` on `syncBus`. One test — emit a drive for the rendered run, assert `sync.drove` was called and that the log grew by the expected event — would pin the seq source, the act wiring and the verdict return in one go.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

- `RunView.tsx:287`: `subjectSuggestions(pack, run.state)` drops the `constrainedBy` argument the page passes (`:1540`), so the offer's suggestions are wider than the page's. Pass the active step's `constrainedBy`.
- `RunView.tsx:2180`: the Attached panel's trailing "Listening, so what the dice say happens in the game. Results still read the same with nothing attached." is tool copy, now shown on a deck-only panel where it is untrue.
- `offer.ts:85`: `owed &gt; 0` withholds even a plain `rollTable` primary (`owed` is `blockingObligations`, unrelated to the step's `extraRolls`), so the follow key goes dead whenever anything is owed anywhere. Conservative and defensible — worth a line in `docs/stream-api.md` so the plugin's key face can say why.
- The publish effect now also re-fires on `receipts.length`/`activeStep`/`pending` changes via `currentOffer`, and every `putSnapshot` rings `changed` to all watchers (`api.ts:2690`), which makes every watcher pull. Roughly doubles the doorbell rate per roll.
- `ws.ts:660`: `tellDecks` runs a `decksOf` query on every watch and every disconnect for every account, deck or not; `heldRuns` is 1 + up to 20 queries per push. Bounded, but it is new cost on a hot path.
- `takePress.ts:60`: a thrown `Error`'s `message` goes straight onto a key face, so an internal `TypeError` text would reach an external client. Tag the intentional ones.
- `memoryLive.connect` keeps previous marks when called with no `attached`; `dynamoLive`'s `PutCommand` replaces the item. Only the deck's re-connect exercises it today.
- No negative test that the publisher stays off with neither `shared` nor a deck — the widened gate would pass its tests if it were always true.
- `activeStepLabel` (`RunView.tsx:76`) is the third copy of the step-heading switch (StepPanel, RemoteStep). It only produces a label, but the label is what a key face promises.

| T11 `connected` not set on `open`; `drive.ts` in no tsc project; 68 lines not 40 | **may ship** — it is a throwaway proof, and it is syntax-checked. |

### Rulings you disagree with

- **Pre-flight, T3↔T7 "agree" on `writes()` as the target set.** `writes()` answers "could this connection take a press", which is not the same question as "which single connection should". Nothing downstream ever narrowed it, and that is Critical #1.
- **Task 9's deferral: "per-contestant moves cannot be driven (Offer.moves erases the contestant)."** The erasure does not make them undriveable; it makes them driveable *incorrectly*. The deferral should have been a filter.
- **Task 11's ruling that "meta.seq is the per-event counter, so it equals events.length."** True at rest, and I verified it — but it omits that the counter only advances after the sync push, which is the Important #6 window. The ruling is right about the identity and incomplete about the timing.

Everything else in the ledger reads correctly to me, including Task 3's swallow-the-whole-body ruling, Task 8's mirror-`needsPlus` ruling, and Task 12's off-list-rows-are-tally-less ruling (the tally arithmetic is right, and the test proves it against a real click).

### Recommendations

1. Settle Critical #1 first — it is a design choice (which writer wins), not a patch. Narrowing to `w.sub === conn.sub` and taking one connection is the smallest change that satisfies the stated constraint.
2. Then the four one-liners: `moves`/`undo` behind `live` (#2), the per-contestant filter (#3), `tellWhoIsAttached` on every watch (#4), and dropping `publishing` from the drive-effect gate (#4).
3. Decide the seq-in-`drove` question (#6) before Plan B starts, and fix the CDK env test (#7) so the gate stays wired.
4. Add the one missing integration test (#9): emit a drive on the bus inside the mounted `RunView` and assert the act and the verdict.

### Assessment

**Ready to merge?** With fixes

**Reasoning:** The design is coherent, the seq contract is genuinely sound, and the act map matches the page button for button — but the server fans a press out to every writing connection, so a second device (or a co-player) applies the same press again and authors it, which breaks both the log and the branch's own "the page is the one writer" constraint; that plus the four cheap seam fixes should land before this merges.</result>

Tests at `hosted/infra/test/ws.test.ts` — the two-device test, the other-member test, and the "Nothing is holding that run." refusal — all present.

**Named check (3):** the double really stores the date — `ws.test.ts` `memoryLive.watch` now takes `at` and writes `watchedAt.set(watchKey(...), at)`, and `watchers()` returns it. The C1 test inserts `laptop` first and `phone` second with a moved clock and expects the drive at `phone`; insertion order would have picked `laptop`, so the sort is load-bearing and the test is not passing by accident.

Test "says so when it cannot check a press, rather than dropping it". `docs/stream-api.md` gains the retry sentence and the new refusal row.

Unmount clears the timer and nulls the ref.

Only two `LiveStore` implementations exist (`dynamoLive`, the `ws.test.ts` double); both implement `unwatch`, and both also clear `watchedAt` on `disconnect`.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

- `apps/web/src/run/RunView.tsx:579` — two presses landing inside one React commit settle the first with `eventCount.current`, which is still the pre-press count, so that first `drove` can name a stale `seq`. Strictly better than the pre-fix behavior (which refused the second press outright) and the second verdict still carries a fresh seq, but the window is not fully closed.
- `apps/web/src/run/takePress.ts:42` — a retried `ref` returns the memoized verdict and appends nothing, so the held verdict can only be answered by the 1500 ms timer; a retry costs the deck 1.5 s. Matches the implementer's own concern 3.
- `RunView.tsx:506-511` — unmount clears a held verdict without sending it, so a page navigating away inside the hold window leaves that press unanswered until the deck's own timeout. Inside the deferred navigated-away case, but the hold window widens it slightly.
- `hosted/infra/lib/handlers/ws.ts:718` — `tellWhoIsAttached` now fires on every watcher join, including race watchers, and posts to every non-control watcher of that id; a room of N watchers reconnecting costs O(N²) posts. No correctness problem (`useAttachedTools.ts:32` filters by run id), just chattier.
- The 1500 ms flush has no test of its own, as the report states.

- ✅ Ruling: everything from a face is escaped — hand-traced below; `esc` (`face.ts:21`) is applied to `title` in each `&lt;tspan&gt;` (`face.ts:62`), to `title` again in `&lt;title&gt;` (`face.ts:70`), and to `when` (`face.ts:64`). No other face-derived string reaches the markup (`tone` is only used as a `TONE` lookup key, never interpolated).
- ✅ `&lt;title&gt;` judged on the merits: **legitimate, not a hack that leaves the visible face unescaped.** Hand-traced `wrap("&lt;Bowl&gt; &amp; \"Kiln\"", 10, 3)` (the actual call inside `faceImage`, default width 10): greedy packing yields `["&lt;Bowl&gt; &amp;", "\"Kiln\""]` — two tspans, so the brief's required contiguous string `"&amp;lt;Bowl&amp;gt; &amp;amp; &amp;quot;Kiln&amp;quot;"` cannot appear in the visible `&lt;tspan&gt;` markup (a `&lt;/tspan&gt;&lt;tspan…&gt;` boundary falls between `&amp;amp;` and `&amp;quot;Kiln&amp;quot;`). The `&lt;title&gt;` element (`face.ts:70`) is what makes test 2's positive contiguous-match assertion satisfiable — this matches the implementer's account exactly. But the visible `&lt;tspan&gt;` text is *independently* escaped (`face.ts:62`, `esc(l)`), and test 2's negative assertion (`expect(svg).not.toContain("&lt;Bowl&gt;")`, `face.test.ts:13`) scans the whole document, including the tspans — if tspan escaping were ever removed, raw `"&lt;Bowl&gt; &amp;"` would appear in a `&lt;tspan&gt;` and this assertion would fail. So the test still guards tspan escaping; `&lt;title&gt;` doesn't substitute for it, it resolves a genuine tension (contiguous-label test vs. truncating-wrap test) that the controller's own ruling says can't both bind one `wrap` width. `&lt;title&gt;` as first child of `&lt;svg&gt;` is standard, spec-legal, invisible-on-render markup (accessible name/tooltip) — placed correctly at `face.ts:70`, before the visual `&lt;rect&gt;`/`&lt;text&gt;` elements.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: actual on-device rendering in Stream Deck 7.5 (visual fidelity, font substitution) — outside what a diff read can confirm; not flagged as a defect, no evidence contradicts it.

### Strengths
- The three brief tests were kept verbatim rather than weakened to fit the implementation (`face.test.ts:5-23` vs. brief lines 20-36) — the stated conflict was resolved by design (adding `&lt;title&gt;`), not by editing the test to relax the check.
- `wrap` termination is structurally guaranteed: `i` strictly increases every outer-loop iteration (both branches at `face.ts:39` and `face.ts:41` increment `i`), so the loop cannot spin regardless of input — including the single-overlong-word case.
- Escaping is centralized in one function (`esc`, `face.ts:21`) applied at every point face-supplied text reaches markup, with ampersand escaped first to avoid double-escaping later-inserted entities.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None found.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None found. Unescaped-text, non-terminating-wrap, and wrong-prefix risks were all checked and are clean (see traces above and in Code Quality below).

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `wrap`'s default `width` is 10, not the brief sketch's 12 (`face.ts:29`). Self-disclosed by the implementer and permitted under ruling 3 ("the sketch is a starting point"), but it's a silent numeric divergence with no test pinning it — a future reader diffing against the brief could be confused.
- Font-size gate at `face.ts:60` (`lines.length === 1 &amp;&amp; face.title.length &lt;= 6 ? 34 : 20`) checks the *raw* `face.title.length` against 6, not the wrapped single line's length, while `wrap`'s width is 10 — so a one-word title of 7-10 characters still renders at 20px even though it occupies exactly one line. Functions correctly, just a conservative/inconsistent threshold; layout taste, not a defect.
- `wrap`'s inline doc comment (`face.ts:23-28`) was rewritten and no longer matches the brief's one-line sketch comment ("Up to three lines of about twelve characters…"). Reasonable under ruling 3 since the implementation itself changed, but flagged since the global constraint text ("the brief's doc comment verbatim") doesn't explicitly scope itself to only the top-level comment.

<result>### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Wire shapes match the server field for field: `watch` `{t,id}` (`streamdeck/src/socket.ts:60`) vs `hosted/infra/lib/handlers/ws.ts:710`; `hello` `{t}` (`socket.ts:80`) vs `ws.ts:284`; `drive` `{t,run,seq,ref,press,move?,answer?}` (`socket.ts:121`) vs the server's read of `m["run"]/m["seq"]/m["ref"]/m["press"]/m["move"]/m["answer"]` at `ws.ts:481-484,543-551`. Nothing else is ever sent — `send` appears exactly three times in `socket.ts` (:60, :80, :121).
- ✅ Inbound branches match: `runs`+`any` (`socket.ts:89` vs `ws.ts:149` `{t:"runs", runs, any}`), `changed` gated on the watched id (`socket.ts:90`), `drove` with `ok`, optional `say`, optional numeric `seq` (`socket.ts:91-98`) mirroring the server's own optional-field construction at `ws.ts:581-589`.

- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: the test/typecheck/build/format runs and the claim that `packages/session` source lands in the minified bundle — not re-run per instructions. The rollup mechanism checks out on inspection (below).

### Strengths
- Frame parity is exact, including the two places it is easy to get wrong: `drive` carries no extra keys because `...p` is a typed two-or-four-field object (`socket.ts:116,121`), and `drove`/`say`/`seq` use the same conditional-spread idiom the server itself uses.
- Reconnect is correct, and for a subtler reason than the code shows. Traced: `onclose` dispatches `socket:"closed"`, whose reducer branch clears `runs: []` and `attached: null` (`state.ts:93`), so on reopen `watching = null` (`socket.ts:78`) and `attachedRun` is also `null` — `follow()` returns early (`socket.ts:57`), no stale `watching` survives, and the re-`watch` is driven by the fresh `runs` push the server sends in answer to `hello` (`ws.ts:284-286`). The doorbell is re-armed on every new socket.
- `press()` reads `store.state.snapshot?.offer?.seq` live at call time (`socket.ts:118`) — no captured value — so the `drove.seq` the reducer folded in is what the next press carries.
- `dispatch` calls listeners only when `reduce` returned a new object (`store.ts:16-18`); `state` is assigned before the loop, so a re-entrant dispatch from a listener reduces off the already-updated state and cannot roll it back.
- `realDeps()` is the only place the globals are touched: `globalThis.WebSocket` and bare `fetch` appear once each, both inside it (`socket.ts:127-135`). `store.ts` and `socket.ts` import nothing from `@elgato/streamdeck`.
- Rollup `include` checked at the mechanism level, not just the report's word: `@rollup/plugin-typescript` builds its filter with `resolve: parsedOptions.options.rootDir || process.cwd()` (`node_modules/@rollup/plugin-typescript/dist/es/index.js:875-883`); `streamdeck/tsconfig.json` sets no `rootDir`, and `build` is `rollup -c` run with cwd `streamdeck/`, so `../packages/**/src/**/*.ts` resolves to the repo's `packages/`. It cannot reach `apps/*` (a sibling of `packages`, not under it). Test files under `packages/*/src` are in the filter but never in the import graph, so nothing extra is bundled.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
- None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- `socket.ts:85-88` — a frame whose body is the literal `null` throws. `JSON.parse("null")` succeeds and assigns `m = null`, then `m["t"]` at `socket.ts:89` raises a `TypeError` that escapes `onmessage` uncaught (the `try` covers only the parse). Every other malformed shape is tolerated (a number or string yields `undefined` on `["t"]`; a missing `runs` fails `Array.isArray`; a missing `ref` fails the `typeof` gate). One `&amp;&amp; m` or a `typeof m === "object" &amp;&amp; m` after the parse closes it. Runlog's own server never sends it, which is why this is Important rather than Critical.
- `socket.ts:89` — `runs` entries are passed through as `never` with no per-item check, and the reducer then does `state.runs[0]!.id` / `r.id === state.pinned` (`state.ts:82-83`). A `runs` array containing a non-object element throws inside `dispatch`, again out of `onmessage`. Same one-line class of fix (filter to items with a string `id`).

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `socket.ts:42-53` — `fetchSnapshot` has no generation guard, so yes: two `changed` in quick succession can land out of order and the older response can overwrite the newer snapshot (`store.dispatch({t:"snapshot"})` replaces wholesale at `state.ts:107`). It does not matter much — the reducer keys nothing on order, a stale `seq` gets refused by the writing device and the refusal's `drove.seq` restores the fresh one (`state.ts:112-113`), and the next ring re-fetches. A late snapshot can likewise clobber a fresher `drove.seq`; same self-heal. A monotonic request counter would remove the window if it ever shows up in practice.
- `store.ts:18` — a listener that dispatches re-entrantly leaves the remaining listeners in the loop receiving the now-stale `next` argument, while `store.state` is already the newer one. `state` is not corrupted, but a listener that trusts its argument rather than `store.state` can render one frame behind. Worth a line of comment, or passing `store.state` instead of `next`.
- `socket.ts:119` — `press()` tests `!socket` rather than `readyState === OPEN`, so it relies on the reducer clearing `runs`/`attached` on `socket:"connecting"` (`state.ts:93`) to keep `attachedRun` null while the socket is still CONNECTING. The invariant holds today, but it lives in another module; a `readyState` check would make it local.
- `socket.ts:55-64` — when the attached run goes away, `follow()` sets `watching = null` and sends nothing, so the server keeps counting this deck on the run it left until a new `watch` arrives (`ws.ts:719-731` only unwatches the previous run on a *new* watch). No client frame exists for the other case, so this is out of the wire spec, not a deviation from it.
- `socket.ts:42-43` — `deps.bearer(account)` is called on every doorbell ring as well as on connect; harmless if `bearer` caches, a needless round trip if it does not.
- `streamdeck/src/plugin.ts:8-9` — the two new imports use `.ts` extensions while the five neighbors above them do not; cosmetic inconsistency only (`allowImportingTsExtensions` is on).

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** Every amended requirement is present and correct, the frames match the deployed server field for field, the reconnect path genuinely re-watches, and `press()` reads the live seq; the only real defect is a narrow `null`-frame throw in `onmessage` that no Runlog server produces and that one guard closes.</result>

<result>### Finding Verdicts

- **A `null` frame throws out of `onmessage`** — ADDRESSED. `streamdeck/src/socket.ts` diff lines: `m` is now parsed into `unknown` (`let m: unknown; ... m = JSON.parse(...)`), and a guard `if (typeof m !== "object" || m === null) return;` runs immediately after the try/catch, before narrowing to `const msg = m as Record&lt;string, unknown&gt;`. `JSON.parse("null")` → `m === null` → guarded return. `JSON.parse("42")` → `typeof 42 !== "object"` → guarded return. Neither throws nor dispatches, matching the two prescribed tests, which the diff adds verbatim (`socket.test.ts` new test "drops a null or bare-value frame without throwing or dispatching").

- **Malformed `runs` entries throw in `dispatch`** — ADDRESSED. `streamdeck/src/socket.ts`, inside the `msg["t"] === "runs"` branch: the array is now filtered with a type predicate — `typeof r === "object" &amp;&amp; r !== null &amp;&amp; typeof (r as Record&lt;string, unknown&gt;)["id"] === "string"` — before being handed to `store.dispatch`. The added test delivers `[{ id: "s1" }, 7, null, { name: "no id" }]` and asserts `store.state.runs` ends up `[{ id: "s1" }]`, matching the ruling exactly.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff

None.

- Object guard vs. arrays: `typeof [] === "object"` and `[] !== null`, so an array frame passes the guard and is treated as `msg` with `msg["t"] === undefined`, matching none of the three branches — it falls through harmlessly (no throw, no dispatch). Legitimate object frames (`{ t: "runs", ... }` etc.) are unaffected by the guard; only `null` and primitives are rejected.
- `runs` filter preserves extra fields: the fix uses `.filter(...)`, not `.map(...)`, so surviving array elements are the original object references — `name`/`packTitle`/`held` (or any other property) on a kept entry are untouched; only the compile-time type is narrowed to `{ id: string }`.
- Empty-array case: `store.dispatch({ t: "runs", runs: runs as never, any: ... })` is called unconditionally after the filter, not gated on `runs.length &gt; 0`, so a frame where every entry is dropped still dispatches `runs: []` — "No run open" still renders. (Not separately tested in the diff, but confirmed by reading the unconditional dispatch call.)
- `any` flag: computed from `msg["any"] === true` against the original message, independent of the `runs` filter result, so it survives regardless of how many entries are dropped.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

None — the diff is confined to the two guarded branches plus their two tests, consistent with both the report's "Left alone, per the coordinator's instruction" list and the stat summary (2 files, 40+/9-, single commit `cfeb496`).

### Verdict

**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage.</result>

### Strengths
- The state/SDK seam is clean: every face string is decided by pure functions in `state.ts` and the only place a key is painted or a press is flashed is `base.ts:64-92`. The six classes are three-to-thirty lines each and say only what their face is.
- `common()` was split into `common()` + `held()` (`state.ts:148-162`) so `connectFace` can skip exactly one rung and reuse the rest — that is why `runFace` shed its duplicated ladder (`state.ts:233-235`) instead of gaining another.

- Launch really is quiet, and the `launched: … not connected` line makes that readable from the log rather than taken on trust.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None found.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **The sign-in failure still looks like nothing happened.** `plugin.ts:110-113` catches, logs the reason, and posts `{t:"who", signedIn:false, apiBase}` — no `error` field; `account.js:52-55` renders `signedIn ? "Signed in" : "Not signed in"`. So the streamer who pressed Sign in sees "Signing in…" (`account.js:38`) flip back to "Not signed in" about 200 ms later with no reason on screen, which is the exact symptom reported from the live attempt. The reason ("this copy of Runlog has no sign-in for a deck", `session.ts:40`) exists and is logged but never reaches the web view. Add `error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : "unknown"` to the failure payload and render it in the `who` branch.
2. **Reconnect can leave an orphaned live socket.** `turnOff()` calls `wire.disconnect()` (`socket.ts:130-133`), which sets `wanted = false` and calls `socket.close()` but leaves `socket` pointing at the closing socket; `turnOn()` then awaits `readGlobals()` before `wire.connect()` (`plugin.ts:40-44`), so there is a window where `wanted` is true again while `socket` is still the old ws and the new one is not yet assigned. A late `onclose` in that window passes the new guard (`socket.ts:118`, which only catches the case where a *newer* socket is already assigned), dispatches `socket: "closed"` and schedules a backoff `open()` — a second connect in flight beside the one `connect()` already started. The loser's `ws` is never closed and keeps dispatching inbound messages for the life of the plugin. A generation counter compared in `open()`/`onclose`, or nulling `socket` and refusing `open()` while one is in flight, closes it.
3. **A trailing slash in the address produces `//api/...`.** `applyGlobals` only trims whitespace (`plugin.ts:60`), and every consumer concatenates: `session.ts:38` (`${account.apiBase}/api/auth/deck`), `socket.ts:69` (snapshot fetch), `socket.ts:78-79` (`${wsBase}/ws?…`). The address the user actually typed — `https://runlog.dev.scrthq.com/` — therefore dials `https://runlog.dev.scrthq.com//api/auth/deck` and `wss://runlog.dev.scrthq.com//ws`, which will not route. Strip trailing slashes in `applyGlobals`.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `base.ts:87-91`: `send()`'s store subscription is removed only when a matching `flash.ref` lands. A press whose verdict never arrives (socket dropped in flight) leaks the listener permanently. A `tick`-based expiry or unsubscribing on the next `on:false` would bound it.
- `account.js:50`: when the device-flow URL is not http(s) the code sets `href = ""`, which resolves to the inspector page itself; hiding the anchor is truer.
- `plugin.ts:56,109,112,120`: the `who` payload carries `apiBase`, but `account.js` never reads it (the field binds through sdpi's global setting) — a dead field, or a missed chance to show which address is being talked to.
- `connect.ts:22-28`: a Connect press with `session === "expired"` skips the alert branch and runs `turnOn()`, which dials, fails the bearer, and flips back to expired. Harmless, but inconsistent with the "none" case one line above.
- `plugin.ts:111` logs the raw error message, which for an expired device code is `"the code expired before it was confirmed; run \`runlog login\` again"` (`packages/session/src/flow.ts:138`) — CLI advice surfacing in a deck's log.
- `run.ts` has no `onKeyDown` while `manifest.json:51` already promises "Press to switch" — expected (Task 11), flagged so it is not forgotten.

### Assessment

**Reasoning:** The amendments are implemented faithfully and verifiably — no auto-connect, exact off-state and Connect faces under whole-object tests, a pure idle rule, a bounded redraw, clean token hygiene, and every SDK name confirmed against the installed 2.1.2 types. Three things block trust: the sign-in failure still never reaches the inspector (the live symptom the controller saw), Reconnect can orphan a live socket, and a trailing slash in the address the user actually typed builds `//api/...`.</result>

<result>### Finding Verdicts

- **Finding 1 — sign-in failure never reached the inspector** — ADDRESSED. `streamdeck/src/plugin.ts:110-117` builds `error` from the caught `Error` (else `"unknown"`), logs it, and sends `{ t: "who", signedIn: false, apiBase, error }`. `streamdeck/com.scrthq.runlog.sdPlugin/ui/account.js:52-58` renders `Not signed in — &lt;error&gt;` when `payload.error` is set, else the plain line. The line clears on the next `who`: `sayWho()` (`plugin.ts:55-57`, invoked from `RunlogAction.onPropertyInspectorDidAppear` in `streamdeck/src/actions/base.ts:65-67`) and the success path (`plugin.ts:109`) both send `who` with no `error` field, so `account.js`'s ternary falls back to the plain "Signed in"/"Not signed in" text.

- **Finding 2 — Reconnect could orphan a live socket** — PARTIALLY ADDRESSED. The specific scenario in the ruling (a late `onclose` from a retired socket) is fixed and tested: `disconnect()` bumps `generation` before dropping its reference and dispatching `closed` itself (`streamdeck/src/socket.ts:137-146`), and `onopen`/`onmessage`/`onclose` all gate on `mine !== generation` (`socket.ts:92,99,124-125`). A current-generation `onclose` still reschedules while `wanted` is true — the `if (wanted) setTimeout(...)` at `socket.ts:128` is untouched by the generation bump for an ordinary drop. Test `socket.test.ts:149-178` covers exactly the connect/disconnect/connect/fire-first-onclose/60s-no-reopen case specified in the ruling.
  Gap found on inspection: in `open()` (`socket.ts:78-90`), the generation guard (`if (mine !== generation) return;`) is placed **after** the `if (!token) { store.dispatch({ t: "session", state: "expired" }); return; }` branch, not before it. A stale `open()` attempt (superseded by a newer `connect()`/`reconnect()`) whose `bearer()` call resolves to `null` late still dispatches `session: expired` unconditionally — and `reduce`'s `"session"` case for a non-`"ok"` state resets to `{ ...initial(), session: event.state, pinned }` (`streamdeck/src/state.ts:107-108`), wiping `on`, `socket`, `runs`, `attached`, `snapshot` even if a newer attempt has already opened a socket. This contradicts the fix's own stated guarantee ("any handler whose number has moved on does nothing") and isn't covered by either new test.
  `fetchSnapshot` (`socket.ts:53-65`) is also not generation-gated directly; it's reachable only through `follow()`, which is gated by the closure's `socket` variable rather than `generation`. In the paths exercised by the tests this is benign (a stale socket's `onmessage`/`onopen` are generation-gated so they can't trigger `follow()`), but a `fetchSnapshot` already in flight when a reconnect happens (kicked off from the prior generation's `onopen`→`follow()`) is not cancelled and will still `store.dispatch({ t: "snapshot", ... })` unconditionally on resolution, using the account/run it captured at call time — a narrow, self-correcting race (the new generation's `onopen` re-triggers `follow()` and refetches), but not literally gated the way `onmessage`/`onclose` are.

- **Finding 3 — trailing slash built `//api/...` and `//ws`** — ADDRESSED. `normalizeBase` (`streamdeck/src/session.ts:264-266`) is `apiBase.trim().replace(/\/+$/, "")`; `"https://x///"` → `"https://x"` as specified. `applyGlobals` (`streamdeck/src/plugin.ts:59-61`) applies it with `|| DEFAULT_API`, so an empty or whitespace-only address falls back to the default rather than `""`. Applied defensively at every join: `session.ts:290` (`clientOf`), `socket.ts:58` (`fetchSnapshot`), `socket.ts:89` (`wsBase`). Tests: `session.test.ts:233-244` (helper directly, trailing/multiple slashes plus whitespace, and already-clean addresses left alone) and `socket.test.ts:181-192` (integration through the getter, one slash in both the `wss://` URL and the snapshot fetch URL).

- **Folded-in Minor — `send()`'s store listener leaked** — ADDRESSED. `base.ts:92-120` bounds the subscription both ways: `!s.on` calls `stop()` silently; a `setTimeout(..., VERDICT_MS)` (5000ms, `base.ts:26`) calls `stop()` and shows the alert if still unresolved. `stop()` is idempotent (`off === null` guard) and cancels the timer via `clearTimeout` when the real verdict arrives first, and clears the store subscription when the timer fires first — so a genuine late `drove` after a timeout alert cannot show `showOk`/`showAlert` twice, and a timeout after the real verdict cannot fire either, verified by reading the mutual-exclusion logic (`base.ts:100-119`). The extra alert-on-timeout behavior beyond a bare unsubscribe is a reasonable, transparently-flagged judgment call and doesn't introduce double feedback.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
- **Moderate — `streamdeck/src/socket.ts:83-87`.** The `!token` early-return in `open()` dispatches `{ t: "session", state: "expired" }` before the `mine !== generation` staleness check, so a superseded `open()` attempt (e.g., two rapid Reconnects, or Reconnect racing a slow token refresh) can still fire after a newer attempt has already connected, resetting `on`/`socket`/`runs`/`attached`/`snapshot` to initial via `reduce`'s `"session"` case (`state.ts:107-108`). Not exercised by either new test. Narrow but real given `bearer()` can involve a network round trip for token renewal.

### Out-of-Scope Observations
- `fetchSnapshot` (`socket.ts:53-65`) has no generation gate of its own; it's reachable only via `follow()`, whose guard is the closure's `socket` reference rather than `generation`. Pre-existing shape (not introduced by this diff — the diff only added `normalizeBase` to its URL), and self-correcting in the tested paths, but worth a follow-up gate if the team wants the same invariant enforced uniformly.
- `flow.ts`'s CLI-flavored error text ("run `runlog login` again") will now surface verbatim in the Stream Deck inspector via Finding 1's fix — implementer flagged this explicitly as out of `packages/session`'s touch list; reasonable to defer.
- The live sign-in path (`GET /api/auth/deck` → 401 on both stages) remains blocked on an undeployed API route, unrelated to this fix round; correctly called out as unchanged.

### Verdict
**Fix round:** Findings remain open — Finding 2 is PARTIALLY ADDRESSED: the specific orphaned-socket scenario from the review is fixed and tested, but the generation guard in `open()` has an ungated branch (`socket.ts:83-87`, the `!token` → `session: expired` dispatch) that can still let a stale connection attempt reset live state. Findings 1, 3, and the folded-in Minor are fully ADDRESSED with matching tests (43→47, confirmed against the diff).</result>

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `nextField`'s handling of the non-wheel `{counter}`/`{resource}` variants (`metric.ts:110`, `typeof field === "string" ? … : -1`) is untested — rotating away from a counter/resource field always lands on `"score"` (index `-1 + 1 = 0`) going forward or `"leader"` going backward. This is a reasonable, documented (comment at `metric.ts:105`) design choice and not required by the brief's test cases or the global constraint's wording, but a one-line test would close the gap between "pure and tested" and what's actually exercised.
- `RunlogAction.redrawAll()` (`base.ts:86-88`) is invoked once per second, for every placed instance of every `RunlogAction` subclass, for as long as any clock anywhere is running — not just the dial(s) displaying that clock's field. This is an extension of the codebase's existing "any state change redraws every subscribed action" pattern (already true for `snapshot`/`runs`/flash-expiry), not a new pattern introduced by this diff, and each `draw()` call itself does only one `setImage`/`setFeedback` — no additional expensive work was added per tick. Worth knowing if dial/key count on a busy deck ever grows large enough for per-second full-deck redraws to matter.

The one unverifiable point (the `$B1` `indicator` key's exact name) is exactly the kind of thing the controller flagged as unconfirmable from any local file, is called out honestly in the report, and does no harm if wrong.</result>

## Issues

**Critical:** none found.

**Important:**
- `apps/web/src/guide/pages/stream-deck.mdx:238` — "Every other key says **Not connected**, with _press Connect_ beneath it, until the connection is open." This overclaims. Per `streamdeck/src/state.ts`, `common()` returns `"Not connected"`/`"press Connect"` only while `state.on === false`. Once Connect has been pressed (`on: true`) but the socket hasn't reached `"open"` yet, `held()` (state.ts:155) returns `{ title: "Offline", tone: "dim" }` — no `"press Connect"` subtitle. So there's a real intermediate state ("Offline") the sentence doesn't account for; "until the connection is open" is not true of the code as written.

**Minor:**
- `docs/stream-api.md:419` — the addition is two sentences ("…says what its keys do." / "What follows is for a deck of your own making.") where the controller's resolution asked for one. The second sentence doesn't restate the guide, so it doesn't violate "one home per fact," but it's a literal miss on the stated constraint.
- `streamdeck/design/category.svg:2` comment ("…which is what the action list wants.") uses the user's flagged `, which is &lt;gloss&gt;` tic. Only one instance, in a source comment rather than guide prose, but worth a pass if this file gets touched again.
- The guide states "Runlog's plugin is in the Stream Deck Marketplace" (`stream-deck.mdx:226`) and the README links to the guide as if live — both true only after the (deliberately out-of-scope) Marketplace submission lands. Self-flagged by the implementer in the report; reasonable to ship docs for the imminent end state, but worth confirming before this merges if the submission timeline is uncertain.
- Undo glyph's arrowhead sits over the arc's end rather than growing tangentially out of it — self-flagged as the weakest of the six icons; reads fine at both sizes but worth a second pass on a real key.
- Commit carries a non-Claude `Entire-Checkpoint:` trailer, which isn't a Claude trailer (satisfies the literal binding constraint) but does contradict the resolution's "No trailers, no footer" — almost certainly appended by session tooling rather than authored by the implementer.

Commit: `39a9bfd` — `feat(run): a deck applies a setup and hands it out` (branch `the-deck-hands-out`, not pushed)

Tests: `npx vitest run apps/web` 693 pass / 97 files (run dir: 251 pass); typecheck, `lint:docs`, and `format:check` on every touched file clean.

Concerns:
1. The brief's `!live` line is self-contradictory ("copies it through unchanged on every path, including `!live`, where it is `[]`"). I took the conservative reading: the `!live` path empties `setups` alongside `moves`/`undo`, since what an ended run was played under is a record `creditLine` states as fact. Reversing it is one word in `offer.ts` plus one test case.

## Strengths

- The `press.html` rewrite of `stripValue`/`show()` is a genuine correctness fix, not just an addition: switching the match from raw string equality to per-option `stripValue` comparison is what lets a setup option match by `id` alone (so a renamed setup still matches a saved target), and the implementer correctly noticed and handled the new edge case this creates — `stripValue(null)` for the "Set up" placeholder option, which the old code never routed through `stripValue`.
- `press.ts`'s `onPropertyInspectorDidAppear` and `onKeyDown` are exact, minimal, literal matches to the brief's specified payloads.

- Docs changes are single-sentence/single-clause, in the existing voice, with no restatement elsewhere.

## Issues

**Critical**
- `streamdeck/src/state.ts:279` — `offer.setups.some((s) =&gt; s.id === target.id)` is not defensive against `offer.setups` being `undefined`, despite the brief explicitly calling this out as a requirement ("optional-safe: an older page may publish no `setups` — check the code tolerates `undefined`"). Since `Snapshot`/`Offer` values arrive over the wire via an unchecked cast (`streamdeck/src/socket.ts:65`, `(await res.json()) as never`), an older page's snapshot genuinely can omit `setups` at runtime regardless of the TS type. Any deck with a Press key configured to a setup target, connected to a page that hasn't deployed the `setups` field, throws inside `pressFace`, which `RunlogAction.draw`/`redrawAll` (`streamdeck/src/actions/base.ts:73,93`) calls with no surrounding try/catch — breaking that key's redraw. Fix: `(offer.setups ?? []).some(...)`.

**Important**
- None beyond the above.

**Minor**
- None worth flagging; the `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer is pre-existing tooling behavior across the whole branch, not something introduced by this task.

## Assessment

**Task quality: Needs fixes.**

The implementation is otherwise tight and faithful to the brief — exact payload shapes, exact tooltip text, correct id-based matching in the property inspector, and every required test present and passing against the clean commit. The one defect is not cosmetic: it's a direct miss of an explicit, called-out brief requirement (runtime tolerance for `offer.setups` being absent), and it produces a real crash path for a plausible deployment sequencing scenario (plugin ships ahead of the page). A one-line fix (`(offer.setups ?? []).some(...)` at `streamdeck/src/state.ts:279`) closes the gap; nothing else in the diff needs to change.</result>

DICE_VERDICT_MS : HELD_VERDICT_MS`). Every other act path leaves `threw` false, so non-dice presses are unaffected. `settleVerdict` nulls `heldVerdict.current` before calling `sync.drove` (`:539-543`), so whichever of {events-length effect, timeout} fires first wins and the other is a no-op — `drove` cannot fire twice for one press's `heldVerdict`.
- ✅ **Tests don't pass vacuously.** `RequestPanel.test.tsx` uses the real `DiceTray` (not mocked); verified `DiceTray.tsx`'s flat-path timer math (`TUMBLE_MS=620` + `STAGGER_MS=90` for one die = 710 ms) matches the test's `FLIGHT_MS = 620 + 90`, and `onSettled` fires from that real timer. Ran `npx vitest run apps/web/src/run` myself: 35 files, 254 tests, all pass.
