4. ✅ `send()`'s four outcomes all tested in `base.test.ts:85-136` (ok, refusal, timeout, deck-off — plus bonus cases for ignored ref and no-wire). `codeFromLines` exported (`plugin.ts:139`) and tested in `plugin.test.ts` against the real `deviceFlow` from `packages/session`, confirmed against the real `say` lines in `packages/session/src/flow.ts:103-107`. `connect.test.ts` and `undo.test.ts` press cases present, shaped like `roll.test.ts`. The "send once" change (`plugin.ts:139-154`, guarded by a `sent` flag) is a real, if modest, correctness/efficiency improvement — it stops re-sending an identical `{t:"code"}` message to the inspector on every subsequent flow line (the blank line and the "waiting" line) after the code and URL are already known; the inspector still receives the code exactly once with both fields, verified by `plugin.test.ts:50-75`. It doesn't fix a data-correctness bug (the redundant sends were idempotent), but it does remove needless postMessage churn/re-render risk in the property inspector — worth keeping.
5. ✅ `when` line: `face.ts:68-69` sets `WHEN_SIZE = 16`, `WHEN_MAX = 13`; `ellipsizeWhen` (`face.ts:77-87`) trims at a word boundary when ≥6 characters remain, else falls back to a character cut; "press Connect" (13 chars) fits whole; "Scenes before a warp" → "Scenes before…" (verified by hand-tracing the algorithm and by `face.test.ts:89-93`). Title shift is `-10` at `face.ts:152`. Tests in `face.test.ts:74-101` match all of this, including a dedicated font-size-16 assertion. `connectFace` (`state.ts:190-196`) matches the brief exactly: Connect (dim, `when: "went idle"` only if `idleOff`), Connecting (dim, covers both the literal "connecting" state and the `on &amp;&amp; socket !== "open"` case), Disconnect (live) with no run name ever — confirmed by `state.test.ts:312-317`, which explicitly presses the case with several runs held and still expects "Disconnect". Guide paragraph at `apps/web/src/guide/pages/stream-deck.mdx:11-15` updated. No "Idle · press to connect" string remains anywhere in shipped code or docs (only in historical `.superpowers/sdd/` planning artifacts, which are not shipped).
6. ✅ All minors: `streamdeck/package.json:2-3` has `"name": "@runlog/streamdeck"` and `"private": true`; `package-lock.json` has matching `node_modules/@runlog/streamdeck` and `streamdeck` entries; `face.ts:1` imports `./state.ts`; `rollup.config.mjs:32` include narrowed to `["src/**/*.ts", "../packages/session/src/**/*.ts"]`; `.github/workflows/CI-CD.yml:509` is `streamdeck/dist/*.streamDeckPlugin`; `manifest.json` has zero diff across the reviewed commits and a clean working tree (not left reformatted).
7. ✅ `9a51d25` touches only `SetupPicker.test.tsx`, adding `configure({ asyncUtilTimeout: 5000 })` with a comment explaining why (busy-worker contention on the picker's first `findByRole`, since it awaits `builtins()`/`setupsHere()`). Nothing else in the file changed (confirmed via `git show`). **View on acceptability**: reasonable as shipped. The report's bisection (green at `cc8150e`/`5aa8d58`, red once the four new streamdeck test files landed, green again at HEAD with just those excluded) rules out the new tests changing `SetupPicker`'s own behavior — this reads as genuine worker-pool contention during a full-suite run, not a regression the timeout is papering over. Raising `asyncUtilTimeout` from RTL's 1000ms default to 5000ms is a proportionate, narrowly-scoped fix for that. It is a workaround rather than a root-cause fix, though — it doesn't address why `builtins()`/`setupsHere()` can take over a second under load, and a genuine future slowdown in those calls would now take up to 5s to surface as a failure instead of 1s. Acceptable as-is; not blocking.
8. ✅ All 7 subjects match the brief/report exactly (verified via `git log`); none of the 7 reviewed commits (nor `cc8150e`) carry `Co-Authored-By` or `Claude-Session` trailers — each carries only the hook's `Entire-Checkpoint:` line.
9. ✅ All commands run clean at HEAD `9a51d25`: `vitest run streamdeck apps/web/src/guide packages/session` → 16 files/123 tests pass; `npm run typecheck` → clean; `npm run lint:docs` → 0 issues/93 files; `npm run format:check` → clean; `npm run build -w streamdeck` → built (same pre-existing circular-dependency and TS5096 warnings the report notes, unrelated to this branch); `streamdeck validate` → "Validation successful". **openFace** — ✅. No target → `{ title: "Set up", tone: "dim" }` (state.ts:339); `guide` lit unconditionally with `tone: "deck"` (state.ts:340); `run`/`rules` pass through `common(state)` first (state.ts:341-343) — verified against `state.test.ts`'s "what the open key says" block.

**Tones** — ✅. `Tone` gains `undo | readout | deck` (state.ts:76). `TONE` in `face.ts:20-27` only adds these three, reusing pre-existing values (`#1e2f27`, `#dd8f63`, `#1b201d`, `#2f3733`, `#151311`, `#8d958f` — all already present in `apps/web/src/styles.css` as `--bg`/`--accent`/`--warn` family or already in the pre-existing `live`/`dim`/`refuse` rows). `live` unchanged and still exclusive to Next/Roll/Press; `undo` reuses `refuse`'s kiln edge on the `live` ground; `readout` is dark ground + hairline (`stroke: 1`) + lit ink; `deck` is dim ground + lit ink. Face functions set tone per family correctly (`nextFace`/`rollFace`/`pressFace` → `live`; `undoFace` → `undo`/`dim`; `metricFace` → `readout`; `connectFace`/`runFace`/`openFace`/`setupFace` → `deck`). `face.test.ts`'s "a tone per family of key" describe block tests each.

**Glyphs** — ✅. `streamdeck/src/glyphs.ts` header states it's written by `npm run icons -w streamdeck` from `design/actions/*.svg`; `design/export.mjs:78-110` performs that write, embedding each SVG's inner markup (not path-only — mixes `&lt;path&gt;`, `&lt;rect&gt;`, `&lt;circle&gt;`, `&lt;g&gt;`), matching the "markup not path data" ruling. `faceImage` (`face.ts:190-192,221`) draws the glyph at 18px (`GLYPH_PX=18`), 8px in (`GLYPH_AT=8`), in the tone's `quiet` color at 70% opacity. `RunlogAction.draw` (`base.ts:99`) calls `faceImage(face, this.glyph())`, resolving the glyph from the action's own UUID suffix (`base.ts:55-57`) — no table to keep in sync. Report's calibration table (x∈[10,23], y∈[9,23]) is inside the ruling's [10,23]×[9,23] bound.

**Hyphen wrap** — ✅. `hyphenated()` (`face.ts:107-116`) finds the longest hyphen-terminated head that fits; `wrap()` tries it before falling back to character-cut (`face.ts:128-134`). One test: `face.test.ts:172-176`, `wrap("Bare-handed", 34) === ["Bare-", "handed"]`.
